| A-M4-T05a | 2026-11-05 | M4.T05 | (a) UP-SD-001 (`Vendor/patches/sdcpp/UP-SD-001-unix-socket.patch`, hook): `--listen-ip unix:<path>` binds an AF_UNIX socket via cpp-httplib; `SDServerSupervisor` launches it in a fresh 0700 directory per launch (no token option exists, so the directory is the auth) and `SDServerClient` talks HTTP over `UnixHTTPClient.fetch` (new chunked collector; no URLSession, no TCP). (c) `SDServerProcessTests` is an opt-in Engines test (`ANTICHAT_REAL_SD=1`, real klein Q4 set) instead of `$XINT`: it drives the supervisor directly. (d) After a crash the supervisor does not respawn by itself: the next job relaunches sd-server (the scheduler owns when it is resident); the relaunch-to-ready time is recorded against NFR-S4. | A-M0-08 | 2026-10-30 | M0.T04 | llama-server router at `620d222` binds the router on the unix socket, but every **loaded model runs in a router-spawned child llama-server that listens on TCP `127.0.0.1:<free port>`** (`tools/server/server-models.cpp:59 CHILD_ADDR, :523-524, :1181`), and the router **strips `--api-key` from the child** (`unset_reserved_args`, `:479-494`), so with `--api-key` the child port answered keyless requests (HTTP 200 on `/v1/models`). Fix adopted: pass the key as the `LLAMA_API_KEY` environment variable (children inherit the router's environment): the child then returns 401 without the key, the router proxy still works, and the key is no longer visible in `ps`. | A-M0-T11a | 2026-10-30 | M0.T11 | Implementation details of the S-13 import prototype: (a) **expected-file format** is per fixture file `{fixture, derived, conversations: [{id, nodes, edges: [[child, parent or null]…], recovered, active_leaf, active_path}]}` (step 1's `{nodes, edges, active_leaf, active_path}` per conversation, wrapped because LibreChat's `chatgpt-export.json` holds 2 conversations and the Claude zip 3; roots are `[id, null]` edges; `recovered` lists re-attached orphans). (b) **usable node** = role user/assistant/tool, not `is_visually_hidden_from_conversation`, non-empty content; ChatGPT browsing calls (assistant `code` to a non-`all` recipient) and results (`tether_browsing_display`/`tether_quote`) are kept as `tool_call`/`tool_result` parts and Claude `thinking` blocks as `reasoning` parts (upstream drops them), so no node with content is lost; system and the null root are dropped and their children re-parented. (c) **repair:** orphan (parent id not in the export) → child of the conversation's first real root, or a root if there is none (LibreChat `chatgpt-citations.json` has none); cycles cut at the first repeated id and re-attached the same way; both set `message.recovered`. Missing/unknown `current_node` → last-child descent from the last root; unusable → nearest usable ancestor. `active_child_id` off the active path = last child; the leaf's is null. Claude conversations with no `parent_message_uuid` at all are chained in array order with `chat.import_notice = flattened_no_parent_ids` (mechanics of the documented fallback; not adopted, unit-tested only). | A-M0-T10a | 2026-10-30 | M0.T10 | (a) **Download chunk size 8 MB, not 64 MB** (M0.T10 step 1 said "8 lanes, 64 MB chunks"; upstream Osaurus uses 32 MB). The vendored chunk map (`.part.json`) records finished chunks only, so a disconnect or kill loses every lane's in-flight chunk, and the re-fetch is bounded by lanes × chunk size. Measured at 50 Mbps with 8 lanes: 32 MB chunks → **231.6 MB** re-fetched (> NFR-D6's 64 MB); 8 MB chunks → 43.6 MB on the probe and, in the gate, 32.7–36.3 MB (disconnect) and 15.9–24.5 MB (kill), 3/3 each. The download was also faster (112.7 vs 143.0 s on the probe). Attempt 1 of the gate's resume legs failed one check because of a spike bug: plain git files (vocab.json etc., 6.8 MB) were re-fetched on every resume. Fixed with a `git-<oid>` → sha256 sidecar, and the legs were re-run (attempt-1 artefacts kept as `*-attempt1`). A network outage (`-1009`, no route) interrupted the first gate run during 10 Mbps run 5; that run was repeated. `ChunkedFileDownloader.chunkSize` becomes a knob (UP-OS-036); the catalog default for M2 is 8 MB with 8 lanes (worst case 64 MB). Sub-chunk resume would allow bigger chunks; it is upstreamable but not needed. (b) **Throttle `[EMULATED]`:** `sudo -n` fails and no Network Link Conditioner is installed, so the documented no-admin fallback is used: one token bucket shared by all lanes (`TokenBucket`, `--rate` Mbps), applied through the new lane byte hook `OsaurusNetworkHooks.laneBytes` (UP-OS-037). The same hook counts the raw received bytes for the re-fetch measurement. (c) Vendored download API made `package` via `access-rewrite.txt` (UP-OS-000 mechanism); `HuggingFaceFileMetadata` is built from the pinned tree API (`lfs.oid`, size) instead of a probe request. (d) CAS under `~/Library/Application Support/AntiChat-Dev/S12/{blobs,manifests,models}` (not `<Models>/blobs`), so clearing it before each run never touches the other dev models. `models/<key>/` is a symlink view for the MLX loader. (e) "Forced disconnect" = every in-flight connection cancelled at ~40 % (`pause()`, then resume from the chunk map). App kill = the bench SIGKILLs itself at ~40 % after saving its byte counter; the relaunch resumes. A 10 s zero-bandwidth stall is reported too (connections survive it, so 0 bytes are re-fetched). (f) The GPU lock is taken only around load + first token, and the lock wait is excluded from the clock (the download is network-only). | Measured re-fetch with upstream chunks fails NFR-D6; no admin for a system throttle; worktree isolation of the dev store. | `build/perf/raw/firstchat-disconnect-32mb.jsonl` (231,598,486 B), `firstchat-disconnect-8mb-probe.jsonl` (43,566,262 B); `sudo -n true` → "a password is required"; `ls /Library/PreferencePanes ~/Library/PreferencePanes` empty; `build/at/AT-M0-10/*`; `docs/research/07-spikes/S-12-first-chat.md`. | NFR-D6 met with the smaller chunk (no budget change); M2 G-051 takes the 8 MB default; more ranged GETs per download (~80 for the 0.65 GB starter vs ~21; K-031 noted). | minor | resolved |

| A-M1-T05a | 2026-10-30 | M1.T05 | Implementation details of the engine contract: (a) **wire shape**: `EngineEvent` is exactly the seven events of step 1; `LifecycleEvent` (loadState, loadProgress, prefillProgress) and the M0 spike media results (`MediaEvent`: voice/image/video) travel beside it as `StreamItem.engine|lifecycle|media` in one `ServerMessage{requestID?, body: helloAck|helloRejected|items([StreamItem], tokens)|probe}` envelope; `tokens` (delta tokens per flush) is a transport field for the bench, so `textDelta(String)` carries no count. Every request stream ends with exactly one terminal (`done(FinishReason)` or `error`); `EngineProtocolVersion.current` = 4. (b) **CR-S-25**: the requestID is decodable on its own (`ServerMessageHeader`), so an undecodable body ends its stream with `engine.undecodableMessage` (all open streams when even the header fails). (c) **CR-S-24**: the listener uses `IncomingSessionRequest.accept { inactiveSession in SessionRouter(…) }`, so the router owns its session before any message (no `SessionBox`). **CR-S-22**: a request is registered in the same critical section that creates its Task; a Cancel also arms a 250 ms guard after which the router itself sends `done(cancelled)` (e.g. while blocked in a model load) and drops later output. (d) **ScriptedEngine**: the Foundation-only script parser/player lives in `AntiChatEngineProtocol/EngineScript.swift` (the app replays in-process under `-UseScriptedEngine` and must not link AntiChatEngines); `Engines/LLM/ScriptedEngine.swift` is the helper `EngineAdapter` around it. The helper selects it per request with `ModelSlot.engine == .scripted` (path = script), because launchd starts the XPC service without the app's launch arguments. Script format, turn selection (segment k = assistant messages in the prompt) and `{{n}}` substitution are documented in EngineScript.swift. (e) **DeltaBatcher** is a small actor anchored at the first pending delta instead of swift-async-algorithms `chunks(ofCount:or: .repeating(every:))` (P-shell-02 `chunked(by:)`): a free-running 16 ms timer flushes 62.5 msgs/s at 200 tok/s (> 60), the anchored window 50.1 msgs/s (DeltaBatcherTests). (f) `AntiChatError` gains `recovery` (decodes M0 payloads without it), the `Code` enum of the 12 M1 codes, the `context` domain, and RecoveryAction cases `restartApp`, `reportProblem`, `continueTurn`, `updateApp` for the copy-deck §8 actions. (g) `EngineService` protocol in Harness (XPC `EngineClient` / `InProcessScriptedEngine`, factory `EngineServices.make`); `EngineClient` connects lazily on the first request; invalidation → `engine.restarted` + reconnect on the next request (T12 owns the choreography). (h) `ClientMessage.probe` → `HelperProbe{pid, residentModels, mediaGateInvocations, sessions}` for R241PathTests (reads `MediaOutputGate.trace` in the helper). (i) **antichat-bench** is a root-package executable target at `tools/bench/Sources/antichat-bench/` that links `AntiChatHarness` (where EngineClient lives; a separate client module would be a module outside architecture §2), never AntiChatEngines; an XPC service is only reachable from its own bundle, so the tool copies itself into `<app>/Contents/MacOS/` and re-execs (use a scratch copy of a signed app). (j) The integration suites link the new `AntiChatHarness` library product (project.yml). (k) Fixture scripts staged in-repo at `tools/fixtures/scripts/` (Q-022). In single-model mode nothing listens on TCP, and the scheduler stays the only thing that loads models (D-013): spawn = load, exit = eviction. This is the task's documented "pre-spawn a standby" fallback. Measured: launchd delays the relaunch of a job that ran < 10 s by the rest of its 10 s ThrottleInterval (10.13 s for a helper killed at 0.5 s). On-demand launch is throttled too, contrary to the step's failure-mode note. `ServiceRotation` picks the job that is not throttled. (d) **Free memory** = `host_statistics64` free + inactive + purgeable + speculative, because `os_proc_available_memory` is `API_UNAVAILABLE(macos)` (SDK `os/proc.h:85`). KV estimate from the model's `config.json`, else 96 KiB/token `[ASSUMPTION]`. `-FakeHardware` T2/T3/T4 use the smallest B of the tier (16/36/72 GB). | A-M1-T09a | 2026-10-30 | M1.T09 | Implementation details of the renderer. (a) **Pins:** MarkdownView `exact: "3.0.0"` resolves to the peeled commit `6f452b5`; the packet's `@4de25d7` is the annotated tag object (same rule as K-NEW-ARCH-4); recorded in `tools/ci/pins.json`. swift-markdown `exact: "0.9.0"` (`25cb61d`, the version MarkdownView's `from: 0.8.0` resolves) is added as a direct dependency of AntiChatUI: parse results are `Markdown.Document`s, and "Copy as CSV" and ⌘⇧; walk them. (b) **Block-split rendering:** the reply is one `MarkdownView` per root block. The block boundaries come from StreamingMarkdownReader's off-main parse. Each block is `Equatable` on its source slice, so while a reply streams only the tail block re-parses and re-lays out. The per-word fade (`WordFadeModifier`, a SwiftUI `TextRenderer`; glyphs are told apart by a `FadeLedger` keyed on glyph advances; opacity 0.2 → 1 over `motion.stream.wordFade` 180 ms) applies to the last two blocks only. The reveal clock runs at 30 Hz, the low end of `motion.stream.cadence` 30–60 Hz. While streaming, the binder pulls the reply text on its own clock (`AssistantMessageView(liveText:)`) instead of receiving every token delta. Stop and interrupted replies reveal everything at once, so the screen still equals the persisted text (A-M1-T06a a). (c) **Mermaid** renders only after `done`. While streaming, the block shows its source as code (the T09 S1 fallback, used by default). The WebView setup: `allowsContentJavaScript = false`, mermaid injected as a user script, a non-persistent store, a block-all `WKContentRuleList`, and a navigation policy that allows only `about:blank` (`MermaidSandboxTests`: `fetch` and `<img>` loads are blocked). (d) **Code block:** label, wrap toggle and Copy are built. The "Save" item in components.md CodeBlock is not in T09's steps and moves with file output (M6). Unknown fence languages stay plain (no highlight.js auto-detect). Highlightr runs one engine per theme on an actor (`xcode` / `atom-one-dark`). (e) **Fixtures staged** in `tools/fixtures/render/golden-100/` (100 `.md` + `SOURCES.md` + 130 PNGs: 100 done goldens, plus 30 `NNN.stream.png` streaming-state goldens for 071–100, where Mermaid shows its source and code highlights completed lines only) and `tools/fixtures/scripts/mixed-3k.jsonl` (13,264 chars, 2,364 deltas at 10 ms). The fixtures submodule is not writable from a worktree (as A-M1-T05a); the lead copies them into the fixtures repo. Mermaid goldens are `WKWebView.takeSnapshot` images (cacheDisplay cannot capture a WebView). Goldens are light-only; light/dark capture stays in VQ. (f) **npm vendoring:** `make vendor-js` (`tools/vendor/vendor-js.py`) runs `npm ci --ignore-scripts` from `Vendor/js/package-lock.json` and writes `Vendor/js/vendored-js.json`. `tools/vendor-sync vendored-json` folds that into `VENDORED.json` (mermaid.min.js sha256 `a8a6a6f8…3b9f`, integrity sha512 from the lockfile). (g) **AT-008 runs in probe form:** XCUITest is blocked (Q-015/Q-024). `App/UITests/RendererStreamingTests.swift` is written and compiles but has not run. The new debug option `-RendererProbe <dir>` (LaunchOptions, M1.md convention 10) and `tools/at/at-008-probe.sh` measure cadence, hitches and fade in-process; the hitch counter is a main run-loop pass > 33 ms (`RendererCPUThrottle`, which also implements `-RendererCPUScale`) until T26a's HitchCounter exists. Layout jumps are measured on the renderer filmed offscreen (`RendererLayoutFramesTests` → `layout-jump.py`; positive control: a 6 px band shift is detected). The live screen recording is informational only: on this shared desktop other apps' windows overlap the crop. (h) **Accessibility:** `assistantText` is now an invisible static-text element whose label is the reply text (TurnTestSupport reads it). The rendered Markdown is the `assistantMarkdown` container. (i) **New copy keys:** `code.wrap/.showAll/.showLess`, `table.copyCSV`, `mermaid.rendering/.error`, `menu.edit.copyLastCode`. (j) **One-line fix in T07's `StartSeparator`:** a static DateFormatter; the transcript re-evaluates that row on every delta, and building a formatter each time cost ≈ 4 % of main-thread time while streaming. | Reuse MarkdownView as P-shell-04 planned while meeting NFR-U5's structure; worktree and XCUITest limits. | `build/at/AT-008/` (`summary.json`, `renderer*.json`, `jumps-frames.json`, `frames-sample/`), `build/at/AT-008-unit/`; time profiles in the T09 report. | none on R-008. | A-M1-T14a | 2026-10-30 | M1.T14 | Implementation details of the prompt layer. (a) The tool block goes to the chat template's native tool slot by default (`ChatRequest.tools` = canonical, compacted list); the Qwen3.5/3.6 templates render that slot at the head of the system turn, i.e. inside the stable prefix, so layer (5) is hashed but not repeated as system text. `ToolPlacement.systemLayer` is the documented fallback for a family whose template puts tools after the system text. (b) Snapshots are `__Snapshots__/system-prompt-{normal,evil}.txt` (the R-241 rule 4 glob, A-M1-T02a d) instead of `normal.txt`; the Evil one uses VISION S-19's wording as a stand-in tone sentence until M2.T17 ships the catalog sentence. (c) `PromptManifest` gets an explicit `public init(sections:)` (UP-OS-038). `promptHash` = the vendored `PromptManifest.prefixHash` (SHA-256, first 128 bits); AT-230 also logs the full SHA-256 of the stable bytes. (d) MLX reuse (`MLXPrefixCache.swift`) keeps exact-prefix KV snapshots only (no trim: Qwen3.5's Gated-DeltaNet caches are not trimmable): one per (model, stable text, tools, thinking flag) at the stable boundary, and one per session (LRU 2) at the end of the history before the generation prompt; requests with images skip reuse; memory-pressure trim drops session snapshots. (e) llama-server: `cache_prompt: true` + `id_slot` per chat (`GET /props` `total_slots`, LRU); slot save/restore for non-incognito chats is deferred to M1.T20 (the request carries no incognito flag yet). (f) TurnController sends one stable session id per chat (it sent a fresh UUID per turn) and stores `provenance_json.promptHash` (new optional `MessageProvenance.promptHash`, additive). (g) `AntiChatHarness` target root moved to `Packages/Harness` (sources narrowed) so the prompts stay at `Packages/Harness/Resources/prompts/`. (h) Observation for the lead: the date line (to the minute) precedes the conversation, so after a minute boundary a hybrid-cache model re-prefills the whole history (only the stable prefix survives); AT-230 measures consecutive turns. **Resolved by D-022** (A-M1-T14b). | Equivalent implementation choices inside the task's design. | `build/at/AT-230/{stability.log,ttft*.json}`, `build/at/AT-269/`, `Packages/Harness/Tests/AntiChatHarnessTests/__Snapshots__/`; template order `chat-9b-q4:chat_template.jinja` L44-60; `make audit` 10/10 ok. | none (no R/NFR/screen change); (h) resolved by D-022 | minor | resolved |

| A-M1-T13a | 2026-10-30 | M1.T13 | Implementation details of the engine conformance suite. (a) **Lazy grammar is the default** (the "grammar slows decode" fallback applied up front): `GrammarBuilder` builds an xgrammar structural tag (MLXGuidedGeneration / MLXCXGrammar at mlx-swift-lm f5b0677, xgrammar v0.1.30): Qwen3.5/3.6 = an EBNF parameter list in any order (each a declared name with a typed value: string enums as raw literals, integer/number/boolean literals, other values raw text up to `</parameter>`; required-presence and duplicates left to the parser and the schema check) — xgrammar's own `qwen_xml_parameter` was tried first and dropped: it fixes the parameter order and renders enum values with JSON quotes, which made the parser reject 16/244 calls and 81 calls schema-invalid in the first AT-231 run; Hermes JSON = `json_schema` and `LazyToolGrammarProcessor` (a `LogitProcessor` via `GenerationComponents.logitProcessorFactory`) masks logits only between the model's own `<tool_call>` token and the grammar's end string, outside reasoning; inactive it reads tokens two steps behind the sampler, so it never waits on the GPU, and the one token after `<tool_call>` (always "\n" in the template) is committed, not masked; the span ends when the stop token is the only one the grammar allows (the stop token is masked inside values). The bitmask → logit mask expansion runs on the GPU (`BitmaskExpander`). xgrammar v0.1.30 has no matcher fork (`clone()` throws), so matchers are reset with `rollback(n)` and pooled. llama-server gets no `grammar` field: with `--jinja` + tools it builds its own lazy tool grammar (`Binaries/llama.cpp@620d222:common/chat-auto-parser-generator.cpp:65-90`); the trace flag `Usage.toolCallsConstrained` counts its streamed calls. (b) **Gemma 4 calls are not grammar-constrained**: xgrammar has no content type for Gemma 4's `<\|"\|>`-quoted call syntax; Gemma rows are parser-validated only (structural validity still gated by the suite). AT-231 runs on chat-9b-q4 (Qwen XML), unaffected. (c) `RawToolSyntaxScrubber` runs once in the helper (`EngineCore.runChat`, all engines) instead of per adapter; it only acts when tools are offered and logs `tool.parseLeak`; `Usage` gains three optional trace fields (`toolCallsConstrained`, `toolCallsRejected`, `rawToolSyntaxScrubbed`). `tool_choice: none` = no tools reach any engine's template (`EngineCore.runChat` strips them; found by the llama row's first run: llama-server renders the tools under `none`, does not parse calls, and the model's call syntax streamed as text, `build/at/AT-229/2026-10-30-evil-T1-run1.log`). (d) **Recovery rows are parser-level**: "malformed-recovery" cannot be prompted from a real model, so 10 of the 50 tool rows feed upstream raw texts (mlx-swift-lm TextToolCallRecoveryTests / NamedJSONToolCallTests) through the family's native parser (ToolCallProcessor `.qwen35` / `.gemma4` / `.json`) + the scrubber in `ToolCallRecoveryConformanceTests` (unit, `build/at/AT-229/recovery.json`); the llama-server row's parser is llama.cpp's own, so its tool score is x/40 with recovery `n/a`. (e) Placement: `EventOrderingTests` is a SwiftPM unit suite in `Packages/EngineProtocol/Tests` (the AT runs it with `make test-unit`), with the checker `EventOrdering` public in EngineProtocol for the integration traces; tool scoring + the eval runner live in `AntiChatHarness/Engine/{ToolCallScoring,ToolEvalRunner}.swift` (shared by `antichat-bench tools` = `tools/bench/Sources/antichat-bench/ToolEval.swift` and the `ToolEval` integration class); the case files are staged in `tools/fixtures/staged/prompts/` (fixtures submodule not writable from the worktree, as A-M1-T05a); `bfcl-subset-100.jsonl` is generated there too. (f) The ScriptedEngine player now emits a `toolCallDelta` before a scripted `toolCallDone` that has none (EventOrderingTests found `tool-loop-runaway.jsonl` violating "toolCallDone after its deltas"). | A-M1-T18a | 2026-10-30 | M1.T18 | Implementation details of context usage and non-destructive compaction. (a) **Summary node placement**: the `system_note` row (`summary_of_ids`, one text part) is appended at the tip of the active path when it is made (the send's parent for the synchronous path, the chat's leaf for idle / retry / edit); a send whose parent's active child is a summary node goes below it (`ChatMessageAdapter.sendParent`). The prompt uses the newest summary of the chat whose covered ids are exactly a prefix of the request's turns (Osaurus `summaryIsValid`), so regenerate / edit / version switches never misuse one and the originals are never touched (no reparenting). (b) **Prompt shape (D-022-compatible)**: covered turns are dropped and the vendored `ConversationSummary.contextMessageText` leads the first kept user turn — not a system message (Qwen templates reject a later system turn, Gemma needs alternation) and not a prompt layer, so the system turn and `promptHash` never change; between compactions each prompt's history is an exact prefix of the next (`CompactionTests.compactionKeepsTheCacheLayout`). The summariser runs under its own session id. (c) **"Appears when first used"**: the reply records the summary in `provenance_json.summary_id` (new optional key); the SummaryNotice is placed once, after the last summarised turn of the newest used summary; it expands to a list of the summarised turns (role icon + first line); the originals stay in the thread above. (d) **Window**: `-FakeContextWindow` > `ModelSlot.contextTokens` > model-matrix tier context (8K for chat-0.8b/chat-2b, else 16K) until M2's catalog; the 75 % / 85 % triggers and the indicator use the vendored `TokenEstimator` estimate of what the next request carries. (e) **Upstream gates scaled to the window**: covered-span token gate min(4,000, window/5) and summary cap min(1,024, window/8) tokens (upstream assumes ≥ 32K windows; at 4K/16K nothing would ever qualify); a reply with `done(length)` is the truncated-summary guard (nothing written, next idle retries); over-cap text with `stop` (the ScriptedEngine ignores `maxTokens`) is clipped on a word boundary. (f) **Ports, not vendored copies** (A-M0-06 (d) keeps ContextBudgetManager/CompactionWatermark/ContextCompactionService in `watch` mode): `Context/ContextCompactionService.swift` is the cited port (cut rule, prompts verbatim, transcript clip, guard); `ContextBudget.swift` ports `estimateTokens(forMessage:)` + the breakdown; the trimmer and the watermark are not ported (AntiChat never drops history silently). `ChatMessageAdapter` maps our rows to the vendored `ConversationSummary` / `TokenEstimator` with no vendored-code patch (DoD). (g) **Waits counted strictly**: a turn counts as waiting when it ran the synchronous fallback or waited for an idle compaction still in flight. (h) **OversizeGuard in three places**: the composer (`ComposerModel.submissionGuard` → `ctx.tooLarge` under the field, text and tray kept), `ConversationModel.send`, and `TurnController.start` (defence; rejected before any row or request). (i) ⌘K FTS skips `system_note` rows (the originals are the hits). (j) `TurnController.start` is `async` (pre-request step: wait/compact); Stop during it cancels the compaction and ends the turn interrupted with no request. | A-M1-T21a | 2026-10-31 | M1.T21 | Implementation details of draft autosave. (a) **Debounce shape**: one `AsyncAlgorithms.debounce(for: .milliseconds(500), clock:)` per window store; every change is recorded as *pending* per key and the debounced pass writes all pending drafts in one transaction (an empty draft deletes its row). Leaving a chat flushes at once, so typing in the next chat cannot push the left chat's write past 1 s. `Storage` gains the `AsyncAlgorithms` product (already pinned 1.1.7 for Foundation; `Package.swift` one line). (b) **Termination flush** via `NSApplication.willTerminateNotification` (posted from `applicationWillTerminate`) observed by `DraftBinding`, not a new `AppDelegate.swift`: M1.T24 owns that file in the parallel W8 worktree. (c) **Incognito seam** (M1.T20's `StoreRouter` not on main yet): `DraftStore(database:isIncognito:)` takes a `(Key) -> Bool` the router binds, and `update/draft/clear(…, incognito:)` take the composer's `ComposerModel.isIncognito`; either keeps the draft in the store's process memory only (`memoryDrafts`, `discardIncognito` on close) — never the `draft` table (tests: flag, seam, window composer). T20's merge wires `router.isIncognito` into `ChatSessions`' store. (d) `attachments_json` = `[{kind: pastedText|file, text?, path?}]`: paste cards keep their text and come back as the same card; file tiles keep the path. (e) **Attribution rule** (`DraftBinding`): a change is saved under the chat the binding shows; a change reported for another chat is a switch (restore, no write), and a value equal to what was last restored/saved is not rewritten, so composer text left from the previous chat never lands in the next one. Clear on send uses the shown chat (or `new`); the new chat's first send leaves neither `new` nor the created chat with a draft. (f) **AT form**: `App/UITests/DraftTests.swift` committed, not run (Q-015; the incognito case skips until T20's ⇧⌘N lands); legs run as `DraftStoreTests` (virtual clock `updated_at − last keystroke`, real clock, restore/clear/empty, flush, incognito flag + seam, cascade), `DraftBindingHostTests` (real MainWindow/LiveComposer in process: switch + card, new chat, send clears, termination flush + relaunch restore, incognito) and the app probe `-DraftProbe` (`tools/at/at-302-probe.sh`: switch; quit = terminate inside the debounce, relaunch; crash = SIGKILL 1.2 s after the last keystroke, relaunch). The AT-302 incognito *disk-diff* leg needs T20's `disk-diff.py` → forward leg (convention 9). | Parallel-wave ownership; T20 not merged; XCUITest blocked. | `build/at/AT-302/` | none (R-302 unchanged) | minor | resolved |

With 4 lanes the vendored finished-chunks-only `PartManifest` loses up to 4 partial chunks per interruption (≈ 128 MB average at 64 MB chunks, failing NFR-D6's ≤ 64 MB); the port keeps an in-chunk **watermark** in `ChunkBitmap` (saved every chunk/16), plus per-instance lanes/chunk size, per-chunk sha256, `sources[]` fallback and 429 → 2 lanes. (a) Views are written in `<Models>/staging/<id>.tmp/` (same volume) and renamed into `installs/`, so `installs/` never holds a `.tmp` even right after `kill -9`; `staging/` is cleared at launch. (b) Placement by engine until the catalog carries `symlinks:` per entry: `mlx-swift-lm` and `llama.cpp` get relative symlinks (M0 spikes loaded symlinked dirs), every other engine APFS clones. (c) Manifests are also stored content-addressed in `<Models>/manifests/<sha>.json`; `model_install.manifest_sha` / `previous_manifest_sha` name them and GC keeps their blobs (rollback window); `models gc --all` drops it. (d) `make models-dev` is **not** re-pointed yet: M0/M1 benches and `test-integration-real` read the old `<Models>/<key>/` layout (`DevModels`), which the scheduler (T12) replaces; `antichat-cli models install --id … --catalog …` exists and is what T12 switches to. (e) Load-time check runs in-process (`Repair.loadCheck`, before the scheduler hands a view to the helper — T12 calls it); RepairTests therefore assert "no crash" for the app process, the helper-PID leg is T12's. (f) `antichat-cli models install` takes model ids (`--id`), not `--tier/--packs`; `catalog sizes --tier` uses the tier's hardware fixture. (g) `--from-store` rebuilds views from installed manifests. (h) `tools/at/at-293-frees.sh` and `at-nfr-d5.sh` are folded into `FreesExactTests` (du delta vs frees, ±1 %) and `BlobStoreTests` (no duplicate sha256 in the store, no regular files in `installs/`); the real T1 Normal + Evil frees run waits for the scheduler-era `models-dev` (d). New key `model.corrupt.redownload` "Re-download ({size})". |
| A-M2-T11a | 2026-11-03 | M2.T11 | Update deviations (minor). (a) `ModelUpdateServiceTests`, `UpdateRollbackTests`, `UpdateTimingTests` run in the SwiftPM unit suite against a local range-server catalog (deterministic), not `test-integration-stub`; the signed `catalog-upgrade*` fixtures drive the diff/policy leg. (b) The Defaults key is `autoUpdateModels` (settings-map-check requires key name = property name), standing for the PRD's `models.autoUpdate`. (c) The "badsha" rollback is exercised as a damaged blob caught by the load gate (`Repair.loadCheck`) and as an engine-rejected first load; a blob whose sha256 is wrong at download never reaches the flip (`download.failed`, T08). |
| A-M2-T12a | 2026-11-03 | M2.T12 | Scheduler deviations. (a) **llama-server router mode is not used** (A-M1-T12a stands: router children always listen on TCP, NFR-SEC6). `LlamaRouterControl` applies RL-005 to single-model servers the helper spawns itself: no model source but the scheduler's `load`, `--sleep-idle-seconds -1`, explicit `--ctx-size` + `--n-gpu-layers 999`, one resident GGUF process (`--models-max` ↔ EvictionPolicy capacity); there is no `POST /models/load|unload`. (b) `ensureLoaded` returns `ModelLeaseToken` (the name `ModelLease` is the vendored Osaurus refcount actor the scheduler counts leases in). (c) Engine capacity is part of fit-or-evict: one MLX container, one llama-server, one scripted engine; a same-engine load replaces the resident model after its leases drain. (d) `SpikeScheduler` became `EngineRuntime` (adapters + media job bodies, the `ResidencyLoader`); every load and every job goes through `ModelScheduler`'s queue. |
| A-M2-T13a | 2026-11-03 | M2.T13 | Step-aside deviations (minor). (a) After a media job the stepped-aside chat model reloads **lazily** at the next user message (step 2, R-236); an `ImageRequest.restoreChat` (the NFR-M3 bench) still reloads at once. (b) ScriptedImageEngine is selected by an `ImageModelSet.diffusionModel` of `scripted:<steps>:<ms>` (DEBUG only). (c) `StepAsideTests` (stub) covers T1 eviction + lazy reload + NFR-R4 and T3 co-residence through the helper; "embeddings kept on T2" and "voice evicted last" are asserted in `StepAsidePlanTests` (unit), because the helper's scripted engine holds one model. (d) The mini-soak has no semantic searches until M2.T16 (no embedding request path yet); `NotEnoughMemoryUITests` is committed, run pending (Q-015). (e) On T3+ the sd-server image context stays after a job and is not a scheduler entry (M4.T04 registers it when the real image pack lands). |
| A-M2-T14a | 2026-11-03 | M2.T14 | Power / pressure deviations (minor). (a) `PowerPolicy`, `ThermalPolicy`, `PerformanceMode`, `PressureLevel` live in `AntiChatFoundation/Power/PowerPolicy.swift`, not `Engines/Scheduler`: the app computes the mode and never links AntiChatEngines, and Platform may not import EngineProtocol (import graph); the helper applies them through `SetPolicy{lowPower, mediaStepDown, thermal}`. `PowerSource` stays in `Downloads/PowerAssertion.swift` (M2.T09); `Platform/Power/` holds `PowerMonitor` and `AppPressureMonitor`. (b) `-FakePressure` reaches the helper as `SetPolicy.testPressure` (Debug); AT-NFR-M4 therefore runs on the Debug helper (valid=false in report.json by the bench rule) and the real `memory_pressure -S -l warn` leg is recorded separately. (c) NFR-B3 pacing is applied by ScriptedImageEngine now; sd-server / video runtimes run their steps internally, so M4/M5 apply it there. Render cadence under Battery saver: the M1 renderer already runs at 30 Hz. (d) A WARNING during a running media job releases idle models directly instead of queueing behind the job (≤ 2 s, NFR-M4). |
| A-M2-T15a | 2026-11-03 | M2.T15 | Mode-swap deviations (minor). (c) `SetPolicy.warmSecondVariant` is tri-state: nil (benches, older senders) keeps the plain budget plan; false forces unload → load of the other chat model even across engines. (d) MLX holds up to two containers when warm (`MLXAdapter.maxResident`). (e) Not run: the NFR-C7 "cold cache" leg (no `sudo -n purge`; [WARM-CACHE]). |

(a) The engine side already existed (M0/M1: `ChatMessage.imagePaths` → MLX `UserInput.Image.url`, llama-server base64 `image_url` parts), so no `MLXVisionInput` / `LlamaServerVisionInput` files and no ChatRequest `.image` part: paths ride on the user message (no bytes over XPC); each adapter logs `vision_parts=N`. | A-M6-T08a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T08 | query_data deviations (minor). (a) Step 3 interrupt path: duckdb-swift keeps its C handles private, so `QueryTimeout.swift` reads `Connection.ptr` once via `Mirror` (pinned @095e4495) for `duckdb_extract_statements` / `duckdb_prepared_statement_type` (the guard) and `duckdb_interrupt` on the dedicated query connection; a renamed field fails closed. (b) The AT's timeout query (`range(1e12) a, range(1e12) b` cross join) never runs 10 s here: DuckDB materialises the build side, hits `memory_limit` and cannot spill (the temp directory is a denied file access), so it fails fast as a tool error (`crossJoinFailsFast`); the 10 s ± 0.5 s check uses a streaming 1e13-row scan. (c) PIVOT needs an explicit `IN (…)` list: an implicit PIVOT extracts as CREATE TYPE + SELECT and is rejected (two statements, with a hint). (d) Result text = a JSON summary line `{columns, data_ref, elapsed_ms, file, row_count, truncated, types}` then the ≤ 200 rows as a Markdown table (rows not repeated in the JSON). (e) `query_data` is in the app registry and offered per step only when the chat has a loaded sheet (`SheetSessions`, filled before each turn's document plan); the call reaches its chat's store through a new `TurnContext.chatID` task-local; a loaded sheet's prompt block is its profile (no page-cite instruction). (f) sheet-qa-50 golds are computed with stdlib + openpyxl (no pandas in the tools env), over `budget-20k.xlsx` (A-M6-T07a (e)) + `notes.csv`. (g) AT-272 3b counts NetworkGate events plus `lsof -i` sampled every 2 s on the bench and helper PIDs (instead of `nettop`). (h) `SheetQueryTests` (XCUITest) is pending (Q-015); the in-process legs are `QueryDataTurnTests` (Harness) and `QueryActivityTests` (UI); vq-07 step 13 waits for the visual QA queue (Q-021). | minor |
| A-M6-01 | 2026-11-04 | M6.T09 | R-273 AC1 says "WebKit PDF"; PDFs are written with TPPDF 2.6.1 (P-files-tools-05), which has running "Page n of N" footers (AC3). User-visible behaviour unchanged. | minor |
| A-M6-02 | 2026-11-04 | M6.T09 | TPPDF pinned at the tag commit `975049c0e1` (2.6.1; `tools/ci/pins.json`), not architecture §9.2's `@222c51f` (RL-005). | minor |
| A-M6-T09a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T09 | create_document deviations (minor). (a) Fonts: bundled Noto Sans/Serif/Mono + Noto Sans Hebrew only (`make vendor-fonts`, OFL-1.1, notofonts.github.io @412a7ed, 5.2 MB). Noto Sans Arabic and the Noto CJK region subsets are not shipped: CoreText's PDF output gives them a broken ToUnicode map (shaped Arabic and CFF-CID CJK text does not extract from the text layer, AC3), and CJK alone is ≈ 23 MB. Arabic and CJK cascade to the macOS faces (Geeza Pro, PingFang SC/TC, Hiragino Sans, Apple SD Gothic Neo), embedded as subsets — the task's documented fallback; no on-demand font component is needed. Bold CJK uses the system bold weights. (b) The PDF Producer is written by an incremental update (new Info object + xref + trailer /Prev): Quartz overwrites any Producer set through PDFKit/CGPDFContext. (c) TPPDF 2.6.1 drops every table row after the first page break when cells carry their own style; tables use only the table style (header/content). RTL paragraphs go to TPPDF's right content container (same flow), because TPPDF sizes text frames to content. (d) RenderHost answers through `callAsyncJavaScript` promise values: no `WKScriptMessageHandler` at all (stricter than "only renderResult/renderError"). WebKit opened a TCP connection for a clicked link / `window.open()` target before the rules cancelled it (RenderHostNetworkTests), so a document-start guard script disables window.open, link clicks and form submits, plus a page CSP `default-src 'none'`. (e) The semantic spec check (`RegisteredTool.validate`) feeds the M1 RepairRetry path, so a schema or semantic problem gets the one repair turn as `{"repair": true, "errors": […]}`; the JSON-Schema checker moved from Harness `ToolSchemaCheck` into AntiChatTools `SpecValidator` (ToolSchemaCheck delegates) and gained oneOf / min/maxItems / min/maxLength. (f) create_document is `.read` (Always allow): it only adds a file to the app's own store. Incognito chats write to `NSTemporaryDirectory()/AntiChat-incognito-out-<uuid>/` (swept at launch). (g) The FileCard shows under the activity line (generating → ready / error + [Try again] = regenerate); M6.T13 moves cards into the turn layout. (h) DOCX tables use fixed twip widths (Pages/Quick Look ignore pct widths); DOCX body fonts are Arial / Times New Roman / Courier New (present on Mac and Windows; nothing embedded). (i) Vendor/js now also carries docx 9.15.0 (IIFE build), pptxgenjs 4.0.1, vega 6.4.0, vega-lite 6.5.0, vega-embed 7.3.0 (T11/T12 use the last four). (j) vq-07 step 14 waits for the visual QA queue (Q-021). | minor |
| A-M6-T10a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T10 | create_spreadsheet / versions / open checks deviations (minor). (a) A sheet takes at most one of `rows` / `data_ref` (not exactly one): a sheet with neither is a header-only template, which T1 makes when the prompt gives no data; a sheet without `columns` takes an all-text first row as its header (types inferred from the values); a chart may name its columns by header ("Region" → A2:A{n}). The data_ref rule is enforced as "a sheet whose headers equal a query result's columns must pass that data_ref". (b) Formula cells carry no cached result (libxlsxwriter); Numbers/Excel compute on open; the app's own XLSX reader sees the inputs. (c) Versions: `revises` (artifact_id, this chat only) on create_document / create_spreadsheet / export_table (and T11's create_presentation); the tool result carries the whole version chain, so the FileCard's ‹ v1 v2 › switcher (FileCardVersionSwitcher) works from the persisted activity; no Library yet (M5.T13). (d) AT-273 check 5 golden leg = the 150 fixed-spec renders (no model): Numbers 60/60, Preview 30/30, TextEdit 30/30 MD + 30/30 DOCX (AppKit OOXML reader), python-docx/openpyxl/pypdf 90/90; **Pages not run (Q-M6-02)**: its first-launch screen asks to accept the Pages licence, which I did not accept on the owner's behalf; Word/Excel absent (Q-M6-01). Model leg: `antichat-bench fileout-golden --limit 6` = 30 of the 150 prompts on chat-9b-q4 at T1 (27/30 files; 1 hallucinated data_ref after its repair turn, 2 answered in text without a call), opened in the same matrix; the full 150-prompt model run (~1 h of GPU on the shared host) is left to M9.T05 with the other re-runs. (e) No `ProcessLauncher` test hook exists in the tree; the bench counts child processes of itself (`proc_listchildpids`, 0) — the writers run in-process. (f) `office-open-check.sh` reads windows and dialog texts through System Events (Automation granted) and classifies ok / repair / error; its full-screen screenshots stay local (gitignored: they show the owner's desktop). (g) The bench's fileout-golden runs under an NSApplication run loop (`runAsyncApp`): WKWebView (RenderHost) does not run under `dispatchMain()`; the release bench needs `-Xcc -DNDEBUG -Xcxx -DNDEBUG` (DuckDB, as the Release app). (h) The PDF incremental-update xref now starts with the free 0-entry (pypdf warned on a non-zero-indexed table). (i) FileVersionTests (XCUITest) is written, pending (Q-015); vq-07 steps 15–16 wait for the visual QA queue (Q-021). K-NEW-FILES-9: no repair dialog in any installed app; K-NEW-FILES-11: libxlsxwriter License.txt = FreeBSD (BSD-2) + bundled minizip (zlib), tmpfileplus (MPL-2.0, unmodified source file), Openwall MD5 (public domain), recorded in ACKNOWLEDGMENTS. | minor |
| A-M6-T11a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T11 | create_presentation deviations (minor). (a) `pptx-golden-20.jsonl` is generated with its fixed specs by `make-fileout-golden.py --formats pptx` (deterministic) instead of hand-written; it covers all 4 themes, all 4 layouts, notes, images, 8 bullets, scripts and the 30-slide maximum. (b) Themes are PptxGenJS slide masters (background, accent bar, slide number) + per-theme fonts (Arial / Georgia / Arial Black — present in Keynote and PowerPoint; nothing embedded) and colours; 16:9 (LAYOUT_WIDE). (c) A slide without a title or an image layout without `image_ref` is tolerated (empty title; the text renders) — T1 often omits them; an unknown image id, > 8 bullets, > 30 slides, a bad layout or theme are validation problems (repair turn). (d) The automatic side-panel open after a deck is made waits for M6.T13 (cards into the turn layout); the card's click/Space Quick Look and the panel's Quick Look body for .pptx are in place (FileOutActivityTests.deckCardAndPanelPreview); PresentationTests (XCUITest) is written, pending (Q-015). (e) AT-275 1c: Keynote shows the same first-launch licence screen as Pages (Q-M6-02, not accepted on the owner's behalf) and PowerPoint is absent (Q-M6-01), so the app open checks did not run; the 20 golden decks re-open in python-pptx 20/20 and render in Quick Look 20/20 (the panel's previewer). (f) The bench resolves `Bundle.module` through the app it re-executes in (`build/AntiChat.app`), so new resources (pptx-themes, the pptx bridge) need `make build` before `antichat-bench fileout-golden`; a first run against a stale bundle failed every deck. Model leg: 5 of the 20 prompts on chat-9b-q4 at T1 → 5/5 decks (python-pptx 5/5, Quick Look 5/5); the full 20 → M9.T05. vq-07 step 17 waits for Q-021. | minor |
| A-M6-T15a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T15 | File tools runtime build deviations (minor). (a) Interpreter = our `bin/ft-python` launcher (`Runtimes/filetools-runtime/launcher/ft-python.c`, `Py_BytesMain` against `Python.framework`) — the task's own fallback: Python-Apple-support's `bin/python3.13` is the python.org framework stub that `posix_spawn`s `Resources/Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python` (two processes and an `.app` inside the runtime); the stub pair is removed. Result: zip 73.4 MB (≤ 90 MB), two clean builds byte-identical (`build/at/AT-018/2026-11-04/`). | minor |

| A-M6-T17a | 2026-11-04 | M6.T17 | AntiChatFileTools.xpc deviations (minor). (a) Spike S-files-sandbox picked option (a): the sandboxed service `posix_spawn`s `bin/ft-python` (app-sandbox + inherit), own process group, env allow-list, CLOEXEC-default. (b) Transport is libxpc dictionaries (`FileToolsWire`: JSON payload + fds) instead of the Swift XPCSession API, which cannot carry file descriptors. (c) New import edge Tools → EngineProtocol (FileToolsClient speaks `FileToolsMessages`, as the task places them) and module FileToolsHost → EngineProtocol (tools/ci/import-graph.py). (d) A TEST build makes Xcode inject testing entitlements into sandboxed targets (`temporary-exception.files.absolute-path.read-only "/"`, testmanagerd mach-lookups, get-task-allow) — found by FileToolsJailTests (the service could read ~/Documents and both databases). Fix: `CODE_SIGN_INJECT_BASE_ENTITLEMENTS: NO` on both services + an app build phase "Seal File Tools entitlements" that re-signs the embedded services with exactly their entitlement files and fails the build if `absolute-path`/`mach-lookup` remain; that phase needs `ENABLE_USER_SCRIPT_SANDBOXING: NO` on the app target (build-time script sandbox only; no runtime effect). The suite asserts the signed entitlements contain no absolute-path, mach-lookup, get-task-allow, read-write or user-selected keys. (e) Residual risks (no escape; S-files-sandbox §Residual): same-sandbox signals, the container (not the chat) is the isolation unit, libc fork/exec allowed by the kernel (limits: M6.T18). | minor |
| A-M6-T18a | 2026-11-05 | M6.T18 | Limits / watchdog / security suite deviations (minor). (a) RLIMIT_CPU: the launcher sets the interpreter's lifetime hard limit 600 s and repl.py lowers the soft limit to "used + 60 s" per run (SIGXCPU → reason timeout), instead of a flat 60 s that a persistent interpreter would exhaust after one minute in total. (b) Watchdog measures the process group ∪ all descendants (catches setsid/setpgid while the parent lives), uses `RUSAGE_INFO_V4` (`ri_phys_footprint`, CPU times converted from mach units), ticks 100 ms and shortens to 20 ms / 2 ms above 40 % / 75 % of the memory cap so a memset-speed allocation is caught within the cap + 5 % bar. (c) New rule: after every reply all other processes of the run are killed (no watchdog measures between runs). (d) Notices: `sandbox.killed` + `sandbox.reason.{memory,fileSize,processes,crash}`, `sandbox.networkOff`, and one extra key `sandbox.denied` for file/process refusals (R-018 AC5 asks for a plain notice; the task listed none for them). MemoryError → memory, EFBIG → fileSize. (e) 04_fork_bomb also runs a raw libc `fork()` loop via ctypes (bypasses the audit hook) so the suite proves the watchdog's process kill, not only the hook. | minor |
| A-M6-T18b | 2026-11-05 | M6.T18 | **Major, open (lead decision; K-051 fallback "hardened option").** The Release-signed services (tools/at/ft-release-probe.sh, `build/at/AT-018/2026-11-05/release/verdict.txt`) deny every probed read, write and socket outside the jail and the Runtimes root (146 probes: ~/Documents, both AntiChat DBs and their /Volumes + /System/Volumes/Data aliases, ~/.ssh, ~/Desktop, ~/Library/Containers, /tmp, loopback connect + bind, DNS) **except the App Sandbox base profile's own read allowances**: `~/Library/Keychains/` (login.keychain-db, metadata.keychain-db readable — encrypted with the login password) and `~/Library/Spelling/LocalDictionary` (also listable: ~/Library/Fonts, Input Methods, Dictionaries). No entitlement removes them, and `sandbox_init` inside the sandboxed service/interpreter is refused (EPERM), so the App Sandbox model cannot close it. Exposure: default variant none off-device (no network; a copy can only reach the user's own chat as an output); the Net variant (opt-in) could upload the encrypted keychain file. Options: (1) accept + document for the Net variant only; (2) hardened option: spawn the interpreter from an unsandboxed launcher that applies a deny-default SBPL profile (major rework of M6.T17). `run_python` stays unregistered (M6.T19 not started) until decided. **Closed 2026-11-08 (M6.T19), no residual:** neither option was needed — the App Sandbox's `com.apple.security.temporary-exception.sbpl` entitlement appends SBPL rules to the profile (last match wins); the four FileTools entitlement files (generated by `tools/release/gen-filetools-entitlements.py`) now carry purely subtractive deny rules: no read under `/Users` (and its `/System/Volumes/Data` alias, and `$HOME`) except the service's own container, its own read-only `.xpc` bundle and the Runtimes root(s); no write under `/Users` except the container; nothing under `/Volumes` or the system/user keychain folders. FileToolsJailTests now asserts EPERM (Python + raw libc) on `~/Library/Keychains/*`, `~/Library/Spelling/LocalDictionary`, the Fonts/Input Methods/Dictionaries listings, the data-volume aliases, `/Users`, `/Users/Shared`, `/Volumes`, `/Library/Keychains/System.keychain` and the host app's own Info.plist, in both variants; `ft-release-probe.sh` re-proves it on the Release-signed services. | major | closed |

(b) The model never sees attachment ids (documents reach it by name), so `run_python.inputs` takes ids **or display names**; inputs land as `in/<display name>` (path-safe, de-duplicated) and the result lists them. (f) Harness additions for AT-276 at T1 (bar and model tier unchanged): every run gets INPUT/OUTPUT paths and a working copy; the same cell re-opens the saved file and checks general formatting invariants (a loss → not saved, the model is told what was lost; `accept_changes` only for an explicitly requested structural change); an unchanged file is not saved; a saved edit lists its changes for the model to check; failures carry the file's outline; `{file}` alone returns the outline. | A-M6-T21a | 2026-11-08 | M6.T21 (close review) | **File tools: per-chat isolation through service slots (lead ruling (c)); the interpreter is frozen between runs.** (a) **Applied.** `FileToolsProtocol.maxSlots` = 3 identical File tools XPC bundles per variant (`AntiChatFileTools[Net]Slot1…3.xpc`, ids `app.antichat.AntiChat.FileTools[.Net].slot<k>`, project.yml template `FileToolsSlot`), each with its own bundle id and so its own App Sandbox container: kernel-level isolation between slots (a nested per-chat seatbelt profile is refused by macOS inside the App Sandbox, EPERM, measured). `FileToolsSlots` (app) leases a slot to one chat; before a lease moves, every variant the slot served is wiped (protocol v2 `ResetSession.wipeSlot`: every interpreter killed, every jail deleted) and must answer `ResetAck.scratchEmpty`; a failed wipe never hands the slot on. A new sbpl deny rule lets a slot write only `Data/scratch` inside its own container and nothing in its darwin user/temp/cache folders, so the scratch wipe leaves nothing of the previous chat. Chats run in parallel up to N (`FileToolsTools.slotCount`): **T0 2, T1 2, T2+ 3**. T0 is 2, not 1: NFR-SEC3's condition "other chats unaffected (a concurrent chat's run_python answers within 2 s)" names no tier, and with one slot a second chat would wait up to 60 s behind a looping run. The per-run caps (1 GB T0, 2 GB elsewhere) are maxima, not reservations: the tier budgets B (hardware-tiers §2) never included the sandbox (one run at its cap already exceeds T1's free budget), typical runs peak at a few hundred MB, and each run uses ≤ 2 threads (A-M6-07), so 2 slots take ≤ 4 cores of a base chip and 3 slots ≤ 6 of a Pro/Max. Beyond N a chat queues (FIFO) and its activity line reads `sandbox.waiting` ("Waiting for a free sandbox…", 5 languages) — a **capacity limit, not an isolation exception**. Every slot of a variant signs with the same entitlements (same deny-only sbpl rules); `gen-filetools-entitlements.py --check` (audit) verifies every slot id, target, seal step and sign.sh mapping, `signing-identity-ids.py` every probe slot id; runtimes are shared read-only, nothing writable is shared. (b) Inside one slot runs are serialised and only the leased chat has a jail; a jail is deleted with its session (kill, reset, idle-out), at service launch and before the idle exit; the app resets chats that ran code on delete, bulk delete, Evil wipe and incognito end. (c) Between runs the interpreter's process group is stopped (SIGSTOP) and continued for the next request; any protocol line that is not the run's reply ends the session. (d) The service starts Python with a bootstrap that sets RLIMIT_NPROC = 1 (soft and hard): the interpreter cannot fork, ctypes included (Limits.swift's note that RLIMIT_NPROC would limit other apps was wrong). Fixture 04 expects denied. | Independent M6 close review (docs/prd/reviews/M6-close-review.md) #1–#11; NFR-SEC3 "no file access outside a per-chat scratch directory" with its "concurrent chat answers within 2 s" condition kept. | `SessionPool.swift`, `Launcher.swift`, `Jail.swift`, `FileToolsService.swift`, `FileToolsClient.swift`, Harness chat lifecycle; `FileToolsSlots.swift`, slot bundles (project.yml), `gen-filetools-entitlements.py`, `sign.sh`, `ft-release-probe` | > N concurrent code-running chats wait for a slot (`sandbox.waiting`) | minor (capacity) | applied |

(d) A near-field onset gate (`levelGateDb`): with no turn open, a frame counts as speech only ≥ N dB above the noise floor (p20 of the last 3 s) — `noise/cafe` babble at −20 dB is speech to Silero v6 and opened a second "turn" after 49/50 utterances; gating every frame instead split turns on soft syllables (15–37/50, tuning-2026-11-07-gate-allframes.json). (e) `TurnSplitFixtureTests` feeds the fixture through the detector in-process (deterministic, faster than real time); the ScriptedVoice → capture-tap path is its own test (`scriptedVoiceUsesInjectedCaptureTap`); the BlackHole leg is pending (P-ADMIN). (a) Barge-in in two steps: the **first voiced frame** (Silero ≥ 0.5, outside the 150 ms post-playback guard) pauses the output in place (TTS silent within one 32 ms frame, NFR-V3), ≥ 250 ms of voiced frames confirm it (flush, cancel the generation, the onset audio stays in the new turn), a shorter onset resumes where it paused (a cough loses no words). Smart-turn filler classification is not used: confirming within the 300 ms cancel bar leaves no room for it, so a filler ≥ 250 ms voiced ("mm-hm") interrupts. (c) `spokenSpan` offsets are UTF-16 offsets into the raw reply; the app stores `TurnController.stop(keepingUTF16:)` (status `interrupted`, the full text never persists). (d) The played clock is the vendored agent-squad `PlaybackClock` fed by per-buffer `.dataPlayedBack` callbacks (100 ms slices) through a new FullDuplexAudioIO hook (UP-SS-007: `schedulePlayback(_:onPlayed:)`, output-only pause / resume / flush); in mode inject a silent clock whose playhead advances only while audio is queued and not paused. (e) Word position inside a chunk is by character share (A-M3-T07a (b)), mapped onto the source words and rounded to the heard word's end. | A-M7-T01a | 2026-11-04 | M7.T01 | Implementation deviations (minor). (a) **HTML parsing:** the ddgs XPaths run verbatim on libxml2's HTML parser through the macOS SDK `libxml2` module (`AntiChatTools/Search/Engines/XPathHTML.swift`), not Kanna 6.1.0 (no new package; Kanna wraps the same parser); Foundation `XMLDocument(.documentTidyHTML)` was rejected because tidy rewrites HTML5 `<a><h2>` blocks. (c) **Startpage port:** one GET with the form's parameters instead of ddgs' `sc` token pre-fetch + POST. (d) **Block rule:** `-SearchBlockEngine` is a transport decorator in AntiChatTools (`BlockingSearchTransport`), not a `NetworkGate` rule; same effect (403 in-process, nothing leaves the Mac). (e) **Proxy recorder:** `tools/offline/proxy-logger.py` + `-TestProxy` (M2.T03) are the recorder; no `mitm-recorder.py` / `-NetworkGateProxy` duplicate. (g) **Catalog:** `search_engines` is an optional top-level catalog key (`catalog/search-engines.yaml` → `catalog-tool build`; `catalog-v2.schema.json` + `search-engines.schema.json`); the app reads it from `CatalogStore.Snapshot.verifiedData`. (h) **Safe search levels:** the user option is on/off, so `safe_param` carries `off` (default) and `on` (each engine's safe value) only; no middle level is sent. (g) **Incognito:** web tools in an incognito chat use the incognito gate session and the in-memory page cache. | A-M7-T03d | 2026-11-10 | M7 bars-3 | **AT-250 c2 scoring rule, pre-registered before the held-out-5 run (lead decision 1, 2026-11-10; final for M7).** m7/bars-2 chose adjudication for T1/T2 after seeing the held-out-4 results; the lead does not accept that as a pass. One rule, applied identically to every tier (T1, T2, T3) and committed (`tools/at/at250-gate.py`, this row) before any held-out-5 capture or run: (1) first pass — every cited sentence labelled by fresh labeller agents (one shard + `tools/at/at250-rubric.md` each; the sentence and the cited source texts only); (2) a full strict second pass over the same set by other fresh labeller agents (same input and rubric); (3) every sentence on which the passes disagree (supported vs not, or n/a vs not) goes to a third fresh adjudicator agent whose verdict decides; sentences both passes call not supported stay not supported; (4) rate = supported / cited (n/a excluded), bar ≥ 0.90 per tier; (5) the strict-only rate (both passes say supported / cited), the per-pass rates and the agreement are reported but do not gate. `at250-gate.py final` refuses to score until the adjudication covers exactly the disagreement set. The held-out set is committed and its sha256 recorded before any capture or run.  **Result (held-out-5, sha256 a2ccc8df…, committed and pushed before capture; m7/bars-3):** T1 chat-9b-q4 **261/289 = 0.903 PASS** (strict-only 0.896, 9 adjudicated); T2 chat-g26 **260/283 = 0.919 PASS** (strict-only 0.905, 10 adjudicated); T3 chat-35b-q4 **208/227 = 0.916 PASS** (strict-only 0.912, 3 adjudicated). Evidence `build/at/AT-250/bars3/h5/`. | Lead decision: a scoring rule chosen after the results is not a pass. | `tools/at/at250-gate.py` | none (R-250 AC2 bar unchanged) | minor | resolved (all tiers ≥ 0.90 under this rule) |

| A-M9-T05-U5 | 2026-11-11 | M9.T05 (U5 renderer fix) | **Renderer rework for NFR-U5 on the S1 stand-in** (M9.T05 AT-008 at `-RendererCPUScale 0.25`: apply median 124.7 ms, 723 main-thread passes > 33 ms, fade frames 0). (a) The reply's root blocks are kept by the binder, append-only (`MarkdownSegmenter`, MarkdownView's stability rule: a block followed by a blank line and another block is final); each apply re-segments only the unstable tail. StreamingMarkdownReader is no longer used: it re-sliced every block into new strings and re-diffed the whole block stack once per clock tick (the fade's `now` went through it) and once per parse commit. (b) Each block is its own observable object; only live blocks (growing or fading in) read the clock, so a tick re-evaluates one or two blocks whatever the reply's length. (c) Live blocks are not selectable until they settle (≈ 0.2 s after their last word; code blocks keep selection): on macOS a selectable Text is drawn by its selection overlay, which ignores `textRenderer`, so the IS-3 word fade never drew on reply text (the probe's 22–46 fading frames per S4 run were the code / table header labels). `-RendererLiveSelectable YES` restores the old behaviour for A/B runs. (d) Reveal clock 40 Hz (25 ms, IS-3's reference), 30 Hz in Low Power Mode; at 30 Hz the apply median sat on the 33.3 ms bound. (e) The streaming row reads `LiveReply.hasText` instead of `text`: the whole AssistantMessageView (and an accessibility label holding the reply) was rebuilt on every 30 Hz flush. (g) Build-window measurements (RendererTickCostTests, offscreen): a selectable streaming reply re-updates every selection overlay per pass (3.7 → 10.4 ms per tick from 12 to 94 blocks), so the streaming reply is not selectable until its reveal settles, then turns selectable 8 blocks per tick; a reply streaming under 60 finished replies cost 56–85 ms per tick (vs 3 ms fresh) because TranscriptList's eager 60-row VStack re-placed and re-dirtied every finished reply's views on each growth — `eagerTail` 60 → 8 (older rows lazy, as before beyond the tail): 4.5 ms. The reveal clock is a sibling view that exists only while a reply animates. (f) Probe: fade frames count fading draws of reply text since the previous display frame (the old check compared the clock's `now` with a 50 ms window, which a ×4-stretched pass never met), plus draw counters. | U5 bar unmet with the old structure; per-tick work scaled with the reply. | `RendererTickCostTests`, `MarkdownSegmenterTests`, `StreamingBlocksTests` (written, not run: quiet-machine window); AT-008 re-run pending. | none on R; NFR-U5 unchanged; transient: text of the block being streamed is selectable once it settles | minor | resolved (lead, 2026-11-11) |

| A-M5-09 | 2026-11-08 | M5 (ChildSafetyCheck call path) | **One bounded re-check after a ChildSafetyCheck deadline timeout** (lead decision on A-M5-07). `ChildSafetyCheck.evaluate(image:)` (used for previews, image results and every sampled video frame): if the check hits its deadline (`timeout_ms` 2000, unchanged), the check waits for the timed-out analysis to finish, then the SAME image is checked once more, both inside one 10 s deadline (no concurrent re-check) (`ChildSafetyCheck.timeoutRetrySeconds`, a code constant, not a calibration value); if that also times out ⇒ block, as before. Only a timeout is retried — a verdict or any other detector error is final. The snapshot stays fixed for a chat's life, except after an explicit Reset: privacy wins over the frozen-prompt rule. (b) **A Debug build on a Mac with the Release app's data** never deletes what both builds share (`Caches/AntiChat`, FileTools containers, `AntiChat*.ips`, `app.antichat.*` Keychain items) in Remove everything or Uninstall. A VM or fresh user has no Release data, so the install suite still removes everything. (c) **Incognito notifications** carry no chat id. A click only brings AntiChat forward. (d) **An AntiChat import** sets project files back to `pending` and re-indexes copied ones from their blobs; linked folders are re-indexed at their next rescan. `db-canon.py --roundtrip` leaves out `project_file.index_state/progress/text_tokens/mtime`. (e) **Relaunch** (restore, Remove everything): the new instance waits for the old process to exit, and the quit skips the job and incognito prompts. After Remove everything it deletes the documented paths once more. (f) **The notification explainer** counts as Not now when no main window is on screen. A quit held by Wait waits at most 5 s for the job's notification. | Review fixes | `docs/prd/reviews/M8-review.md`; `make test-unit`; AT re-runs in `build/at/` | R-301, R-060, R-093, R-108, R-205, R-218, R-226 (AC unchanged) | minor | resolved |
| A-M8-T16b | 2026-11-08 | M8.T16 | Close fixes from the install suite and the PR3 re-run (minor). (a) **Preferences after removal:** `defaults delete` of a populated domain leaves an empty `<domain>.plist` (cfprefsd), and `KeyboardShortcuts.reset` writes every default combo into the domain. The uninstall waiter therefore runs `defaults delete` before it removes the files. Remove everything runs login item, shortcuts and notifications first, then the Keychain, the domain and the files. (b) **`Logs/AntiChat` in Debug:** the vendored UncaughtExceptionLogger always writes `Logs/AntiChat/last-crash-reason.log`. A Debug build lists that folder beside `Logs/AntiChat-Dev` and leaves it alone when the Release app's data is on the Mac (as A-M8-T16a b). (c) **Login item check:** `SMAppService.unregister()` turns the app's BTM record to `disabled`. backgroundtaskmanagementd keeps the record, and no API removes it. The install suite fails only on an AntiChat record that is still **enabled**, and reinstall still requires `loginItem=notRegistered`. (d) **Second window at launch (real bug):** the +1 s "open a window if none" check ran before the scene's own window was ordered in on a busy Mac or in a background run, so the app opened two windows. At launch, any window of the `main` WindowGroup now counts. This was also the cause of the PR3 entry-point failures and the open incognito-session count: the probe's clicks and palette landed in the second window. (e) **Probe ⇧⌘N:** ⇧⌘N opens a new incognito window (M1.T24). The probe checks that window, then closes it. An app that is not active gets no key window, and AppKit matches no synthetic ⇧⌘N to the menu, so the probe runs the menu item that carries the ⇧⌘N key equivalent. The toolbar button and the palette run in the probe's own window. (f) **PR3 isolation:** `disk-diff.py --own <box>` reports content-free changes in other `Application Support/AntiChat*` folders as `foreign` instead of violations. | A-M1-T14d | 2026-11-10 | M1.T14 (AT-269 test fix) | **How `PrefixReuseTests/testToolBlockReuse` measures "the token length of the stable prefix".** The AT-269 pass condition is unchanged (turn 2 `usage.cachedTokens ≥` the token length of the stable prefix including the tool block, both engines; turn-2 TTFT p50 ≤ NFR-C1; tool block bytes identical). The test took that length from turn 1's `cachedTokens` (what a new chat reuses from the previous one), but pair 0's turn 1 was a byte-identical repeat of the warm-up request (same question, same minute's D-022 time line), so on llama-server it restored the end-of-prompt context checkpoint (n − 4 = 872): the 835-token stable prefix plus 37 tokens of the first user turn. Turn 2 cannot reuse those (the time line moves off that turn, so the renders diverge at 847 < 872) and correctly restored the last-user-message checkpoint at 835 = the whole stable prefix, which failed the test's 835 ≥ 872 check on both b11669 and b12254. Fix (test only): the warm-up uses a different first message, so pair 0's turn 1 is a new chat sharing only the stable prefix like pairs 1–19; the "≥ half the manifest estimate" floor now also applies to pair 0 (the exemption is gone), and the measured stable prefix must be equal across all 20 pairs. The bar is stricter, not looser. Not a product bug: no reuse the criterion covers is lost. Observation (outside AT-269, AT-230's domain): on hybrid models llama-server can only resume at a checkpoint, so turn 2 re-prefills the previous user message's text (12 tokens here), because the time line moved off it. | Pair 0 failed with stable 872 / turn-2 cached 835 on both llama.cpp builds | `build/at/AT-269/root-cause-2026-11-10.json` (failing run, `/apply-template` + `/tokenize` check: user-turn suffix 41 tokens → 876 − 41 = 835; divergence 876 − 29 = 847), `build/at/AT-269/reuse-{evil-T1,T1}-debug.json` (passing runs), `Binaries/llama.cpp@45c8ea8:tools/server/server-context.cpp` (checkpoints at the last user message and at n − 4; restore needs `pos_max ≤` divergence) | none (test measurement only; R-269 / AT-269 text unchanged) | minor | resolved |

| A-M9-T03b | 2026-11-11 | M9.T03 (AT-223 part 3, test method) | **How `AccessibilityAuditTests` checks element-less Parent/Child mismatch issues (S-21 per section and scroll position, S-18).** They are counted, not failed one by one, and the count must lie in **[switches, attributable]**, all counted by `tools/ci/a11y-axstate` from the app's exact pid (`ANTICHAT_A11Y_PIDFILE`): at least one per SwiftUI labels-hidden `.switch` Toggle in the window tree (each reliably produces one; repro `tools/a11y/repros/labels-hidden-switch-parentchild.swift`), at most switches + a focused text field + AppKit-backed progress indicators + the quick-panel hotkey recorder's orphaned clear button (`labelsHiddenSwitch`, `progressIndicator`, `quickPanelRecorderClear` in `tools/a11y/audit-ignores.yml`). Fewer than the switches, or any issue beyond the attributable total, fails. Rationale: every element-less issue is still explained by an AppKit/SwiftUI-owned element we can name, and our own code adds none; the audit's internal merging of these sources is not ours to model. Onsets: Montreal Forced Aligner 3.4.3 (MIT, conda-forge; `english_us_arpa` acoustic model 3.0.0, dictionary 3.0.0, G2P 2.0.0a, CC BY 4.0; installed, pinned and sha256-checked by `tools/voice/setup-mfa.sh` = `make mfa` = bootstrap step 11b), each played chunk aligned to its own text (`tools/voice/word-onsets.py`); independent of the product (Kokoro's durations are never read) and of ASR emission times. | A-M9-T05-F7 | 2026-11-12 | M9.T05 (fix/t05-f7, NFR-F7 T1) | **ChildSafetyCheck age stage: one call per image, CPU preprocessing in parallel, and the age bins (Accelerate) run beside the MiVOLO runs (ONNX Runtime) of the same image.** Narrows the M4 rule "every model run holds one lock" (commit `401e9c8be`) to one lock per engine: ONNX Runtime runs (Freepik, Marqo, MiVOLO) still never overlap each other anywhere, age-bin forwards never overlap each other, evaluations still run one at a time on the process-wide lane; only inside one evaluation may one age-bin forward run while a MiVOLO run is in progress (and, at load, the age-bin warm-up forward beside an ONNX warm-up run; activations of a few MB). Same models, files, thresholds, rule, stages, crops, tensors and deadline; fail closed unchanged (any error or timeout in either half while nude ⇒ block; both halves always finish before the check returns, so a timed-out check still holds the lane until it ends). One stricter edge: a non-finite MiVOLO age from any estimator is now an error (⇒ block while nude); MiVOLOAge already threw on it. Not done: batching several faces into one MiVOLO run (the pinned graph's input is fixed `[1, 6, 384, 384]`, no symbolic batch dim; batching would need a converted model, D-007), MiVOLO runs in parallel (the M4 lead removed them), Vision beside Freepik (≤ 5 ms, and it would overlap a CoreML session run). | F7 T1 misses p95 ≤ 300 ms (305.5 ms; earlier runs 314.1, 299.8). Fit on the per-image medians: 0-face images ≈ 65 ms (nudity + Vision), + ≈ 30 ms per face query (MiVOLO, serial), + ≈ 73 ms per body-only person (MiVOLO ≈ 30 + age bins ≈ 43; image 010, no face, 138 ms) — all in series. | NFR-F7 T1 (expected p95 ≈ 225–285 ms, centre ≈ 240, from the fit: the bins of 001/002/029 hide behind MiVOLO, ≈ 2–4 ms CPU preprocessing per query leaves the serial path; 004 stays ≈ 330–360 ms, the worst-image p95 stays above 300); R-285 / D-006 unchanged; `Packages/ChildSafetyCheck` README "Fails closed". | A-M9-T05-VID2 | 2026-11-12 | M9.T05 (video.T4 after round 60; VID2) | **(1) Video step previews are decoded in chunks (runtime 0.3.5). Detectors, thresholds (`preview_min_side_px` 512, `timeout_ms` 2000), sampling, fail-closed rules and "nothing shown or written before allow" unchanged: every shown preview frame is checked at 931×512 by `MediaOutputGate.checkPreview` before it is written or announced; the final frames as before. (1) Since 0.3.4 a T4 preview (512 px short side) is decoded at full resolution; one-shot over its 8 latent frames the TAEHV graph ran at 1280×704 × 29 frames beside the resident DiT. Now `frames.StreamingTAEHV` (exact, carried MemBlock state) one latent frame at a time, keeping only the 8 used frames on the host, with MLX's freed-buffer cache dropped before and after the preview (measured at the T4 preview shape, runtime interpreter: one-shot 10.0–10.4 GiB MLX peak and 16–22 GiB left in the cache until the denoise ends → chunked 2.83 GiB peak, 0 GiB left; 0.7–0.9 s per preview incl. JPEGs; frames = the one-shot decode up to float rounding of the per-chunk convolutions, mean |d| ≤ 7e-4, a single chunk is bit-identical). Whole T4 default job in the runtime interpreter (under soak load): 0.3.4 preview path MLX-held peak 39.7 GiB at the step-3 preview (denoise peak 15.43 GiB, = the T05 rows) → 0.3.5 22.9 GiB (decode stage, unchanged) and denoise peak 13.64 GiB; with 2/3: previews at steps 2 and 3, the first 49 s into a 73 s job. (2) With 0.75 the only preview of a 3-step job is step 3's, which arrives with the end of the denoise (T4: 64.8–73.0 s into a 67.7–76.0 s job): no preview is ever seen before the video. Step 2's x0 prediction arrives ≈ 2/3 into the denoise. A detector block still stops the job. (3) The bench read previews only from `.videoProgress.previewPath`, which no engine sets, and gave video jobs no preview folder: `preview_events` was 0 and `first_preview_s` −1 by construction. It now gives each job a preview folder (as MediaJobQueue does) and counts the runner's `jobProgress` with a preview file (the event the card shows); the `videoGate` note carries `preview_shown`; run.py's F7_VIDEO notes print shown previews and the first one's lead over the video. | T05 worktree: `build/at/T4-video/t05-t4-default.{log,jsonl}`, `events-t05-t4-default.jsonl`, `build/perf/raw/m1/F7_VIDEO-T4-20261112T055526Z.jsonl`, `build/at/AT-290/{t05,t05-postmerge}/video-speed-*.jsonl`. | A-M9-T05-AT273 | 2026-11-12 | M9.T05 (fix/at273-fileout; AT-273 check 5, AT-275 check 1c) | **File output: near-valid calls are normalised into the requested file, never a file of another format.** (1) Before the schema check, the file-out tools (`create_spreadsheet`, `create_document`, `create_presentation`) rewrite the shapes a T1 model emits (`FileOutNormalizer`): a list sent as a JSON string (also missing a closing bracket), one object for a list, strings for column / image objects, records for rows, `column_widths: ["auto"]`; chart refs as headers / literal value lists / a chart-only sheet → the data sheet's ranges; formulas keyed by a column name → cells (a labelled total row, or a per-row column); image refs that do not resolve (`photo1`, alt text, `photo_<id>`, an invented name) → the chat's attached pictures (id inside the ref, file name, number, next unused), else a visible "*[Image not available: …]*" line; an invented `data_ref` in a chat with no query result → the sheet keeps its columns. (2) The format the user's message names wins inside a tool (CSV vs Excel; PDF vs Word vs Markdown): a CSV keeps the prompt-named (else first) sheet and drops other sheets, charts and formulas; a format another file tool writes is an error and a repair to that tool, never a file. (3) Every change is told to the model with the result ("Adjusted to make this file: …"), to relay in one sentence. (4) A spreadsheet whose every sheet is empty is rejected (was an empty file). (5) The repair turn of a file call carries the exact errors and a minimal valid call of that format (with the chat's real picture ids), no "or answer without it", and the next step is forced to that tool (`tool_choice: function`). (6) A step that may call a file tool for a message asking for a file gets max(step budget, min(8192, window/2)) output tokens (the app default is 1024); the cut-off notice names a size that fits (≤ 40 rows / ≈ 1,200 words / ≤ 12 slides). (7) The turn context lists the pictures sent in the chat with their ids ("Pictures attached in this chat: …") whenever a file tool is offered — the model never saw them before. (8) Tool schema text: `sheets` is an array never a string, CSV = one sheet without formulas / chart, formula keys are cells, image refs are attached-picture ids. Bench: `fileout-golden` sends the photo attachments with the message like the composer (`.send(attachmentIDs:)`); its draft rows never resolved, which caused all 13 AT-275 failures and the image documents' "no attachment or file with id" (the product lookup is right to ignore unsent drafts); the ids are no longer pasted into the prompt. | T05 Phase B round 60, T1 chat-9b-q4: AT-273 121/150 (check 5 needs 150/150), every failure a failed call then a text answer; AT-275 7/20, all 13 failures `image_ref: no attachment or file with id` of the bench's own ids; csv-24 silently made an .xlsx for a CSV request. | Replay of all 44 recorded failing calls (`Packages/Tools/Tests/AntiChatToolsTests/Fixtures/fileout-t05-failures.json`, `FileOutNormalizerReplayTests`, `AttachmentLookupReplayTests`): 25 of AT-273's 29 now valid specs of the requested format; md-16 / pdf-16 / xlsx-16 (and csv-16's first call) were cut off with no arguments (the larger file budget + sized cut-off notice is the path); xlsx-18 (and csv-24) are a deliberate "every sheet is empty" repair; 13/13 AT-275 decks valid once the photos are sent with the message. Harness: `FileOutRepairTests`. | R-273 AC1/AC2 (repair turn), R-275; AT-273 check 5, AT-275 check 1c re-runs (`files.spreadsheet`, `files.presentation`); FileOutContext; RepairRetry prompt for file tools only. | minor | proposed |
| A-M9-T05-B5b | 2026-11-13 | M9.T05 (fix/b5-attribution, AT-NFR-B5 round 66) | **GPU-yield sampler: 1 s cadence, 9 s yield hold, culprit naming without WindowServer.** (1) `GPUYieldMonitor` samples every 1 s (was 2 s); `GPUYieldDecider.yieldHoldSeconds` = 9 s, counted from the start of the first interval over 50 %, so the yield fires 8–10 s into a sustained load at any phase against the samples (was 10–12 s before the UI). The resume rule (< 30 % for ≥ 80 % of 10 s, quiet at both ends) is unchanged. (2) WindowServer's own-UI baseline is its share over the first 4 s of intervals in which no other non-own process uses ≥ 5 % of the GPU (before: the job's first two samples, which an early load could inflate); with none yet, the lowest share seen. (3) The named process is the non-own, non-WindowServer process with the largest sustained rise over its own quiet-time share during the busy episode (≥ 5 % on average, up in ≥ half the intervals); with none, no name (logs `busiest=-`; the card's copy is the generic `video.paused.app` either way).
